(g) Replace the whole `### When motion or WebGL is unavailable` section (its heading and both paragraphs, lines 404–408) with:

```md
### Three levels

| Level | When | What the guest sees |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **1. The book** | A day link, motion allowed, WebGL, a screen at least 640 px tall, and the book ready within 6 seconds. | The 3D pop-up book in the mood's attic. |
| **2. Cards over the live attic** | Motion allowed and WebGL, but the screen is shorter than 640 px, the book was not ready in time, or the screen turned too short during the book. | The flat room's cards and signposts in front of the live 3D attic of the mood: its props and sky, and no book. |
| **3. The paper diorama** | Reduced motion, no WebGL, a lost WebGL context, an error starting or running the 3D, or level 2's scene not arrived yet. | The flat room's cards in front of layered paper cut-outs of the mood's attic. |

- **The load timeout:** the flat room opens on the page the guest was heading to, over the paper diorama. The 3D keeps loading; when it arrives, the live attic fades in behind the cards over about 600 ms, and the paper diorama under it stops moving. Nothing on the cards moves, and focus stays where it was. The guest stays in the flat room for the rest of the visit.
- **A short screen at start:** level 2 directly. The 3D starts loading at page start for these guests too.
- **A lost context or an error** at level 1 or 2: level 3 on the current page, with no retry for the rest of the visit.
- **Level 2:** the live attic sits behind the cards, takes no taps, and is hidden from screen readers. It renders at a lower pixel ratio. The flat room's sideways position drifts and turns it gently, so it moves with a little parallax; vertical moves do not. The cards stay opaque paper, and no text sits on the 3D.
- **Level 3:** the back wall with the round window and the mood's moon or sun, the rafters, the star garland or the lantern string, the shelf, crib, chair, and lamp in silhouette, and the floor slide at different rates as the flat camera moves sideways. Its colors follow the mood and theme. Plain HTML and SVG, with no canvas.
- **Low-end devices:** a device with 2 cores or fewer, or 2 GB of memory or less, gets no real shadow map, a lower pixel ratio, about half the stars, fireflies, and dust, and slower ambient motion. Everything else is the same.

`prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` gives level 3, still: no parallax and no canvas. Every layer moves at the camera's speed, so there is no depth. Cats are still pictures, with no line boil. They do not roam, scratch in a loop, or throw things on their own. A tap on the countdown or the QR may play one small settle, then stop. `Saludar` still works, with the same noticing rules, and so does the battle, with no wipe. Signposts jump to their landmark instead of gliding. Every page's object is already open. The stage still follows the camera position. The family simply stands in the door. The gate shows no game.

Whenever the flat room takes over from the book, it opens on the page the guest was on, or was heading to (the page nearest the camera's target), not on Llegada, and it stays for the rest of the visit, with every landmark that belongs at that clock. An open dialog (**Tus gatos**, a card, the battle) stays open through the change. With motion allowed, the flat room keeps its depth layers and its roaming cats.
```

## Plan notes

These refine the controller's task boundaries; none changes what the spec asks for.

1. **The mood reaches the World in Task 1, not Task 2.** Task 1 must update the callers of `palettes[theme]`; without the day, `World` would have to hard-code a mood and Sunday would briefly render Saturday. `WorldProps.mood` and `moodOfDay[day]` in `BookPages` therefore land in Task 1; Task 2 keeps the lighting, the sky, and the tier plumbing. For the same reason, Task 1 replaces the stage lerps of the key and ambient light with the mood's values (a compile fix, since `WorldLook` loses `{ boceto, luz }`), and Task 2 moves them into `MoodLights` behind the tested `moodLight` helper.
2. **The tier is read in Task 2, and counts scale where they are built.** `WorldRoom` reads `deviceTier(navigator)` once per visit from Task 2, so the stars (Task 2), the fireflies and dust (Task 4), and the motion speed take the tier where they are made, each with its test. Task 8 applies the renderer-level parts (no shadow map, the pixel-ratio caps through `bookCanvas`/`backdropCanvas`), the readout, and the low-tier integration test.
3. **The live attic ships in the World chunk.** `Backdrop` is re-exported from `src/world/World.tsx` and `loadBackdrop` picks it from `loadWorld()`, so spec §7's "the World chunk keeps loading; when it arrives, level 2 fades in" holds literally, the timeout path fetches nothing new, and the "one lazy World chunk" check stays simple.
4. **In the book the Familia card drops its whole SVG door, not only the CSS family.** Spec §6 says the card's flat SVG door is replaced by the 3D doorway and figures, framed above the card. So in the book (`familyInWorld`) the card keeps the star, Misha, Kira, and the line; the frame tap moves to the 3D door piece (an R3F `onClick` that plays the same `mishaScratch` act, so Misha's scratch pose plays; the SVG scratch marks exist only where the SVG door is), and the Cuento completo glow moves to the 3D doorway (`applyStageMix(..., complete)`).
5. **The 3D family stands above the card.** drei's `Html` pages always paint over the canvas, so anything behind the card's rectangle is hidden. The figures therefore stand `standY` up the doorway, just above the card's top, sized to fit the door piece; `familyWalk.test.ts` pins that they fit.
6. **The draw-call budget test uses the spec's measured baseline.** jsdom cannot run frustum culling, so the static test adds the new content's exact calls (derived from the factories) to `budget.bookAtRest` (47, from spec §8) and `budget.attic` (27, the attic's meshes and its window after Task 2). Task 8's dev readout is the runtime check in the browser.
7. **`roomLevel` takes `backdropReady`.** Spec §10 lists "the backdrop not ready yet" as a `roomLevel` condition (it answers `diorama`), so a second helper, `wantsBackdrop`, decides whether to load level 2 at all.
8. **The window's stars count as the sky, not an ambient prop motion.** Saturday moves the mobile and the dust; the garland and the moonbeam hold still. `ambientMotions` pins the rule.
9. **Sunday dusk's sky is a two-stop gradient** (plum at the top, a candle–rose mix at the horizon), which passes through rose between them; spec §3 describes it as candle → rose → plum.

## What could not be grounded in the code

- **iOS Safari's `navigator.hardwareConcurrency`.** Resolved by a controller ruling before execution: the core count only counts where the browser also reports `deviceMemory` (Chromium), so Safari and Firefox are always normal and an iPhone never drops to low on its core count alone. Low devices are simulated with `deviceMemory` in tests and in the browser check.
- **The 3D door tap covers the whole door quad**, doorway included, because three.js raycasts the plane, not its alpha cut-out.
- **Visual values are starting values.** Prop placements, look colors, the family's `standY`, and the diorama drawing are judged in the screenshot steps; the tests pin structure and the camera corridor, not taste.
- **The throttled timing depends on the machine.** On a fast machine the throttled book may still make the 6 s limit; Step 8 says what to do then.
